(91)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{16\pi G}\int_{\partial\mathcal{M}}(-1)^{d-2}B_{IJ}\wedge\Delta^{IJ},

(92)

where
\mathchoice{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.84679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60484pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{KL}}}}{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.84679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60484pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{KL}}}}{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.84679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60484pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{KL}}}}{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.84679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60484pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{KL}}}}
are the components of the curvature two-form
\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{IJ}=\frac{1}{2}\mathchoice{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.84679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60484pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{KL}}}}{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.84679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60484pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{KL}}}}{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.84679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60484pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{KL}}}}{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.6959pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.84679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60484pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{KL}}}}e^{K}\wedge e^{L}
.

Since we can always find a reference connection
\accentset{\bullet}{\Omega}^{IJ}
such that
\accentset{\bullet}{\nabla}_{a}p^{I}=0
and
{R}^{IJ}[\accentset{\bullet}{\Omega}]=0
, we can now introduce the following teleparallel action, which is quadratic in the
\mathfrak{an}_{d-1}
Lie algebra-valued field strength
\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{T}}^{I}
,

S_{\text{\it tele}}[e,\accentset{\bullet}{\Omega},p]=\frac{1}{16\pi G}\int_{\mathcal{M}}d^{d}v\,\bigg[\frac{1}{2}\mathchoice{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{T}}^{{{K}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{IJ}}}}{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{T}}^{{{K}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{IJ}}}}{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{T}}^{{{K}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{IJ}}}}{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{T}}^{{{K}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0906pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{IJ}}}}\mathchoice{\accentset{\bullet}{\mathcal{T}}^{{{IJ}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2229pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72687pt (93)

V
Outlook

We have shown in this paper how the canonical transformation induced by the boundary volume term was similar to the canonical transformation considered in
Dupuis:2020ndx
to recover the notion of quantum group for gravity in three-dimensions. The canonical transformation results essentially in a shift of the connection by the frame field,

A^{IJ}\,{\rightarrow}\,\Omega^{IJ}=A^{IJ}+e^{[I}p^{J]},

(94)

where
p
is either interpreted as the normal to the boundary or as a constant vector. In both cases, the norm of
p
is constrained to be proportional to the cosmological constant.
This is true in any dimension higher or equal than three. In two dimensions, the frame field does not appear in the
B
-field, so the shift cannot be done by such boundary term. This can also be seen from the normalization condition
p^{2}=-\frac{8\Lambda}{(d-1)(d-2)}
.

Figure 1:
Brief summary of the paper: First of all, we performed a change of variables going from the spin connection to a shifted connection
\Omega
. The new connection
\Omega
depends on a boundary field
p
, whose norm determines the cosmological constant. Integrating out the internal Lorentz transformations, we obtain a new second-order metric formulation, which resembles unimodular gravity (the norm of
p_{a}
is determined by the cosmological constant). A non-Abelian version of teleparallel gravity is found by introducing a specific flat reference connection
\accentset{\bullet}{\Omega}
with
\mathrm{d}_{\accentset{\bullet}{\Omega}}p^{I}=0
.

We would like to emphasize that the change of variables can also be performed when
\Lambda=0
. In this case,
p
will be a null vector in the Lorentzian case. In the Euclidean case, we can use a Grassmanian number
\theta
to parametrize
p
such that
\theta^{2}=0
.

We identified the two associated second-order theories, namely the metric and the teleparallel formulations, see the figure above. The metric formalism associated to the new variables can be seen as a new version of the Henneaux-Teitelboim model for unimodular gravity. We note that the first-order action (
60
) can also be interpreted as a first-order formulation for unimodular gravity, since it can be supplemented with the normalizing constraint of the vector
p
,

\displaystyle S_{\text{EC}}[e,\Omega,p]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi G}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Big(\Big(\frac{1}{(d-2)!}\,\varepsilon_{IJK_{1}\cdots K_{d-2}}\,e^{K_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge e^{K_{d-2}}\Big)\wedge R^{IJ}[\Omega]

(95)

\displaystyle-\frac{(d-2)(-1)^{d-2}}{(d-1)!}\Big(\varepsilon_{IK_{1}\dots K_{d-1}}e^{K_{1}}\wedge\dots e^{K_{d-1}}\Big)\wedge\mathrm{d}_{\Omega}p^{I}-\frac{1}{2}g_{ab}p^{a}p^{b}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\,

As in the metric formulation, variation with respect to
\tau
leads to the norm of
p
being constant, hence recovering the cosmological constant as a constant of integration.
The teleparallel formulation, on the other hand, is now expressed in terms of a non-Abelian field strength (
85
) that enters the action quadratically (
93
). With respect to the usual Abelian teleparallel formulation, the Abelian Lie algebra
\mathbb{R}^{d}
is deformed into the non-abelian Lie algebra
\mathfrak{an}_{d-1}
.

The results of this paper summarized in Fig.
1
opens a number of interesting new directions to explore. The boundary volume term plays an important role in the holographic renormalization context
Bianchi:2001kw
. It would be interesting to see whether the formulation using the new variables can also be useful, in particular, its connection with unimodular gravity.

The boundary term is the key to understand why quantum groups appear in three dimensions
Dupuis:2020ndx
. In the four-dimensional context it has been conjectured that they should also be relevant
Smolin:2002sz
;
Fairbairn:2010cp
;
Han:2010pz
. It would then be important to study how the charge algebra is affected by adding this term to the theory. In particular, in the list of works
Freidel:2020xyx
;
Freidel:2020svx
;
Freidel:2020ayo
, it was always assumed that
\Lambda=0
. It would be worthwhile to study how these results are affected if
\Lambda\neq 0
and the boundary volume term is also considered.

The non-Abelian formulation of teleparallel gravity is likely to be the classical continuum counterpart of the (deformed) dual BF vacuum
Dittrich:2016typ
. It would be interesting to construct the discrete picture, which leads upon quantization to such a deformed dual BF vacuum, generalizing
Delcamp:2018sef
;
Dupuis:2019unm
. This is work in progress.

The origin of this work came from studying three-dimensional gravity. Recently, a most general bulk action for three-dimensional gravity was introduced
Geiller:2020edh
. It would be interesting to see how the change of variables could be of use there, either for discretizing or in recovering the different second order formalisms.

Finally, the canonical transformation is implemented for a constant homogenous curvature, parametrized by the cosmological constant. One might wonder whether we could generalize the construction in the case of a varying curvature. We leave this intriguing question for later investigations.
